Extensions, Deferred Payment 
Agreements, and Balanced Billing 
Objectives 
By the end of this module, you will be able to: 
1. Review an account to determine extension and payment 
plan eligibility. 
2. Offer and activate a deferred payment agreement. 
3. Enroll a customer on Balanced Billing. 
 
Granting an Extension 
An extension is a special condition that is added to a customer’s 
account.  Conversation CACE allows you to add a special condi-
tion to a customer’s account.  The extension appears in CRIS as 
STOP FIELD ACTIVITY. 
 
Note: It is important to understand that an extension will not stop 
any collection-related activity except the Field Representative will 
not visit the premises to Collect or Lock the Meter. All other collec-
tion-related activity continues. 
 
The code SFACT appears in the banner and in conversation IQCS 
if the customer currently has an extension. 
 
Credit Extension Eligibility 
 
Residential Customers 
 
All residential customers are eligible for up to a 30-day extension 
unless they requested an extension within the prior 6-months or 
they are required to earn an extension. 
 
Requested Extension 
 
Residential customers may request an extension at any time.  If 
they request an extension they are not eligible for another exten-
sion for 6-months. Use common sense and good judgment when 
determining whether a customer is eligible for another extension 
before you refuse the request. 
 
 
 
 
 
Notes: 
 
 
 
 
Testimony of William D. Yates, CPA, Cases 23-G-0225, 23-G-0226 
Exhibit ___(WDY-05) 
Page 2 of 220


--- Page 3 ---
Requirement to Earn an Extension 
 
Residential customers, who defaulted on a Specific Agreement 
within the prior 18-months and are unable to pay the reinstate-
ment amount, must earn an extension.  
 
Note: If the customer must earn an extension, the payment must 
be made BEFORE the extension is given. 
 
Time Requested 
Payment Percentage Required 
 
1-week extension 
30% of the arrears (termination balance) 
2-week extension 
40% of the arrears (termination balance) 
4-week extension 
50% of the arrears (termination balance) 
 
After the payment is accepted, the extension is added to the ac-
count.   
 
If the customer wants to mail the payment or bring the payment 
into a Customer Office, do not add the extension.  Notate the ac-
count and indicate in the remarks field the amount and the method 
of payment. 
 
Extensions Not Requested by the Customer 
National Grid may add an extension to a residential customer’s 
account to complete needed activities.  If National Grid added the 
extension within the last 6-months but the customer did not re-
quest the extension, the customer is still entitled to the requested 
extension. 
 
Examples of Extensions not requested by the customer are: 
• 
Active Attorney Referral 
• 
Result of HH (Hand Held) 
• 
Result of Matrix Activity 
 
This information appears in the Remarks field in the Detail screen 
of conversation AHCL for an entry of ADD FIELD ACTIVITY STOP. 
 
Residential customers may be granted an extension of up to 30 
days.  If the customer needs more than 30-days to pay the bal-
ance, you need to determine if the customer is eligible for a DPA. 
 
Non-Residential Customers 
 
Non-Residential customers may be eligible for an extension of up 
to 1 billing period if they have not had an extension for any reason 
within the last 6-months.  
 
Monthly-billed non-residential customers may get up to a 30-day 
extension. Bi-Monthly billed non-residential customers may get up 
to a 60-day extension.

Conversation CACE - Add Special Condition 
Conversation CACE allows you to add a special condition to a 
customer’s account. The most common type of special condition is 
a credit extension. Conversation CACE has 2 screens, these in-
clude: 
Credit Status screen 
Add Special Conditions screen 
 
Conversation CACE – Add Special Conditions – Task List 
 
 
 
The Credit Status screen (PF01) is conversation IQCS. This con-
versation has been covered previously. The Add Special Condi-
tions screen (PF02) is used to add the special condition. 
 
 
 
Notes: 
 
 
Testimony of William D. Yates, CPA, Cases 23-G-0225, 23-G-0226 
Exhibit ___(WDY-05) 
Page 4 of 220


--- Page 5 ---
Conversation CACE – Add Special Condition Screen (PF02) 
 
 
 
This screen requires codes when selecting the Type of Special 
Condition. Field Level Help (?) is available. 
 
Description of Fields 
 
Type of Spec Cond – This is a code entry field.  Type the appro-
priate code for the special condition that you are adding. 
 
Note: Code 0008 – STOP FIELD ACTIVITY is used to add an ex-
tension for both residential and non-residential customers for the 
field eligible date. 
 
Expiration Date – This is an entry field.  Type the date that you 
want the extension to expire in this field. 
 
Note: The date that you enter in the Expiration Date field will be 
displayed as the Field Eligible Date in conversation IQCS.  
 
Employee ID – This is an entry field. Type your 5-digit employee 
ID number in this field. 
 
Department/Requested By – These fields are protected and you 
cannot enter any information in them. The information will be rec-
orded based upon your 5-digit employee ID number. 
 
Remarks – This is a free form message field.  Type remarks 
based upon the reason that you are giving the extension. 
 
Select PF16 to process.  Since Conversation CACE adds special 
conditions that may delay our ability to collect the A/R, the enter 
password screen appears.  Type your CRIS password and hit en-
ter.

Conversation CADS – Update Special 
Conditions 
Conversation CADS allows you to update a special condition on a 
customer’s account. Conversation CADS has 2 screens, these 
include: 
Credit Status screen 
Update Special Conditions screen 
 
The Credit Status screen (PF01) is conversation IQCS.  This con-
versation has been covered previously.  The Update Special Con-
ditions screen (PF02) is used to update the special condition.  In 
the Outbound Collections section, this conversation is generally 
used to add additional time to an extension. 
 
Conversation CADS – Update Special Conditions Screen 
(PF02) 
 
 
 
This screen is prefilled with the special condition that exists on the 
account.  If more than 1 special condition exists, a screen is dis-
played listing all the existing special conditions.  You would need 
to select the special condition that you want to update. 
 
Description of Fields 
 
Type of Spec Cond – This field is prefilled with the special condi-
tion that you want to update. 
 
Date Expiration – This field is prefilled with the date that the exist-
ing special condition expires.  This is an entry field, type the new 
expiration date. 
 
Employee ID – This is an entry field.  Type your employee ID 
number. 
Notes:

Department/Requested By – These fields are protected.  The

Remarks – This is a free form message field.  Type appropriate 
remarks with the reason that you are updating the special condi-
tion. 
 
Action – This is a code entry field.  Type the appropriate code for

Granting a Deferred Payment Agreement 
Customers may be eligible for a deferred payment agreement 
(DPA) to pay their balance. HEFPA and the PSC Rules provide 
regulations for eligibility and terms for a DPA.  If a customer is un-
able to pay their bill and if eligible, an extension is not sufficient to 
pay the balance, review the account to determine if the customer 
is eligible for a DPA. 
 
A DPA is an agreement between National Grid and the customer 
for payment of their outstanding balance. 
 
Eligibility for a DPA 
 
Residential Customers 
 
All residential customers are eligible for a DPA unless the custom-
er defaulted on a specific agreement within the prior 18-months. 
 
Non-Residential Customers 
 
All non-residential customers are eligible for a DPA unless they: 
• 
Currently have a DPA. 
• 
Defaulted on a DPA for any reason within the prior 12 
months. 
• 
Are a publicly held company (or subsidiary of a publicly 
held company). 
• 
Are seasonal or short-term customers. 
• 
Have a consumption of 4,000 therms or more within the 
prior 12 months. 
 
Note: A non-residential customer is considered short-term (or 
temporary) if they request service for up to but not exceeding 2-
years.  A non-residential customer is considered seasonal if they 
apply for and receive service periodically, intermittently, or at ir-
regular intervals throughout the year. 
 
Determining if a DPA is Defaulted 
 
Conversation IQAG provides information about current or prior 
agreements.  To determine if the customer defaulted on a DPA, 
you need to review conversation IQAG and conversation AHCL. 
 
 
Notes:

Conversation IQAG - Agreement Inquiry 
Conversation IQAG provides information about current or prior 
DPAs.  If there is a history of more than one agreement, an 
agreement list screen is displayed.  Select the agreement you 
need to review from the list, if applicable. 
 
Conversation IQAG – Agreement Inquiry Screen 
 
 
 
Description of Fields 
 
Agreement Data 
 
Agreement Type – This field displays the type of agreement.   
 
Note: Residential customer accounts will display either 
SPCHF (Specific HEFPA) or GENHF (General HEFPA).  
Non-Residential customer accounts will display Other. 
 
Agreement Status – This is a code field that displays the status 
that is associated with the selected DPA. 
 
Agreement Date – This field displays the date that the DPA was 
made.  This is not necessarily the same date that the customer 
accepted the DPA. 
 
Downpayment Due Date – This field displays the date that the 
downpayment was due.  
 
Agreement Due Date – This field displays the date that the 
monthly DPA installment is due. 
 
Number Installments – This field displays the number of monthly 
installments that are needed to complete this DPA. 
Notes:

--- Page 12 ---
 
Number Reinstates – This field displays the number of times that 
this DPA was reinstated after a default. 
 
Agreement Amount – This field displays the dollar amount that 
was included in the DPA. 
 
Downpayment Amount – This field displays the dollar amount for 
the DPA downpayment. 
 
Installment Amount – This field displays the dollar amount for the 
DPA monthly installment. 
 
Payments to Date – This field displays the dollar amount of all 
payments applied to the DPA. 
 
Installment Due – This field displays the code and decode for the 
installment schedule.  All National Grid DPAs are scheduled for 
monthly installments. 
 
Received Signed Agreement – This is a Y/N (Yes/No) field indi-
cating whether National Grid received the signed DPA from the 
customer. 
 
Note: If a customer defaulted on a DPA, review conversation 
AHCL to determine the date of the Defaulted DPA. This will help 
you determine if the customer is eligible for another DPA.

Offering a Deferred Payment Agreement 
After you review the account to determine if the customer is eligi-
ble for and needs a DPA, you need to explain the following infor-
mation to the customer. 
 
• 
The customer must pay the DPA downpayment on or be-
fore the downpayment due date.  This date is available in 
conversation CAAG. 
• 
The customer must pay the DPA installment amount each 
month on or before the installment due date.  This date is 
available in conversation CAAG. 
• 
The customer must pay all bills issued after the current 
date (today’s date) in full, before the bill becomes past due. 
 
DPA Terms – Residential Customers 
If the residential customer is eligible for a DPA, the terms that can 
be offered appear in the Request Agreement screen (PF03) in 
conversation CAAG.  If the Request Agreement screen does not 
display terms for the agreement, the residential customer is not 
eligible for the DPA. 
 
CRIS provides 2-types of DPAs for residential customers, they 
are: 
 
Specific HEFPA DPA – The downpayment is 15% of the balance.  
The remaining balance is paid over a 10-month period.  The bal-
ance may be paid in less than 10 months if monthly installments 
would be less than $10.00. 
 
General HEFPA DPA - The downpayment amount is provided by 
the customer and  is more than 15% of the balance.  The remain-
ing balance is paid in less than 10 months. National Grid does not 
offer a General DPA to the customer. If the customer has a gen-
eral DPA, the customer provided the terms for the DPA. 
 
Note: If this DPA is requested, supervisor approval is required to 
establish this DPA.  If approved, create an ASRQ stating, “SUP 
OK DPA TERMS”. 
 
Residential customers must be offered Balanced Billing if they ac-
cept a DPA. 
 
Extended Payment Plan – Offered for Active Accounts only and 
customers are only eligible for one extended payment plan per 
calendar year (January – December).  The downpayment amount 
is 10% of the balance.  The remaining balance is paid over 18 
months if the total account balance is less than $1500.00.  If the 
total account balance is $1500.00 or greater, the remaining bal-
ance is paid over 24 months. 
Notes: 
 
 
 
 
Testimony of William D. Yates, CPA, Cases 23-G-0225, 23-G-0226 
Exhibit ___(WDY-05)

Note: If the residential customer claims a hardship, the customer 
can go into one of our District Offices or go online to our website 
to complete a 3596 form to see if they qualify for a minimum DPA. 
https://www.nationalgridus.com/Financial-Statement-Calculator-
NYC  
 
DPA Terms – Non-Residential Customers 
If the non-residential customer is eligible for a DPA, they can be 
offered an OTHER agreement.  The terms that can be offered are: 
 
Downpayment – The downpayment amount is determined by the 
Collection Field Visit status of the account.  If National Grid visited 
the premises to Collect or Lock the Meter, the downpayment is 
50% of the arrears.  If National Grid did not visit the premises to 
Collect or Lock the Meter, the downpayment is 30% of the arrears. 
 
The number of monthly installments available for a non-residential 
customer cannot exceed 6-months. 
 
Accepting the Downpayment Amount 
 
If the customer is eligible to use check payment by phone and you 
offer a customer a DPA, ask the customer if they want to pay the 
downpayment amount using check payment by phone. If the cus-
tomer wants to pay the downpayment now, process conversation 
CSTC. 
 
All DPAs are processed using conversation CAAG.

Conversation CAAG – Agreement Request 
Conversation CAAG allows you to review, add, or update an 
agreement with a customer.  Conversation CAAG contains 4-
screens, they are: 
• 
Credit Status (PF01) – This screen is conversation IQCS. 
• 
Update Agreement (PF02) – This screen allows you to 
change an existing agreement or to reinstate a defaulted 
agreement  
• 
Request Agreement (PF03) – This screen allows you to 
add a new agreement. 
• 
Agreement Inquiry (PF04) – This is conversation IQAG. 
 
Conversation CAAG – Agreement Request Task List Screen

Conversation CAAG – Request Agreement Screen (PF03) 
 
 
 
The terms that can be offered to the customer appear in this 
screen.  If the customer wants terms that are easier to pay then 
the terms displayed in this screen, they must be referred to the 
Customer Office or web to complete a Financial Statement (Form 
DSS3596). 
 
If the customer is referred to a Customer Office, cancel (PF24) out 
of conversation CAAG and process conversation ASRQ or ASCN 
to fully notate the account. 
 
Description of Fields 
 
Agreement Data 
 
Agreement Type – This field displays the type of agreement. 
CRIS automatically determines the DPA type that is offered to the 
customer. 
 
Agreement Date – This field displays the current date.  
 
Dwnpmt Due Date – This field displays the date that the down-
payment is due.  
 
Agrmnt Due Date – This field displays the date that the monthly 
DPA installment is due.  This date is 30 days in the future indicat-
ing the date that the 1st installment is due. 
 
No Installments – This field displays the number of monthly in-
stallments that are needed to complete this DPA.  This field can be 
changed if the customer negotiates other terms.

Agreement Amount – This field displays the dollar amount that is 
included in the DPA. 
 
Downpayment Amount – This field displays the dollar amount for 
the DPA downpayment.  This field can be changed if the customer 
negotiates other terms. 
 
Installment Amount – This field displays the dollar amount for the 
DPA monthly installment.  This field can be changed if the cus-

Installment Due – This field displays the code and decode for the 
installment schedule. 
 
Note: All National Grid DPAs are scheduled for monthly install-

Received Signed Agreement – This is a Y/N (Yes/No) field pre-

Mail Agreement – This is a Y/N (Yes/No) field prefilled with N

code is selected to mail the agreement. 
 
Remarks – This is a free form message field.  Type any appropri-
ate remarks concerning the DPA.

the action that you are selecting.  Field Level Help (?) is available.

Updating an Existing Agreement 
 
An agreement may be updated by using conversation CAAG 
PF02.  If an agreement exists against the account, this screen can 
be accessed.  This screen can not be accessed if the customer 
had a defaulted agreement and it is passed the point of being re-
instated or if the customer has never had an agreement offered to 
them in the past.  Options to update an agreement include:  ac-
cepting, reinstating, updating to reflect receiving a signed agree-
ment, and terminating the agreement. 
 
If the customer defaulted on a DPA and is eligible to have the DPA 
reinstated after payment is received, you need to access the Up-
date Agreement screen to process the reinstatement.  
 
Note: If the customer wants the DPA reinstated, they must pay the 
amount due before the DPA is reinstated.  
 
Note: If the customer wants to renegotiate the DPA, the customer 
must visit a Customer Office with the appropriate documentation 
or fill the Financial Hardship application on the web. 
 
Renegotiating a DPA 
 
A DPA can be renegotiated if the customer has a significant 
change in their financial circumstances for reasons that were be-
yond their control and the change happened after they signed the 
DPA. 
 
If the customer is eligible for a renegotiated DPA, refer the cus-
tomer into a Customer Office or to complete the Financial Hard-
ship application on the web and advise the customer that they 
must provide of the financial change. 
 
Reinstate a DPA 
 
If a customer defaults on a DPA and they are eligible to reinstate 
the DPA, you need to access conversation CAAG – Update 
Agreement screen (PF02) to get the amount that the customer 
needs to pay. 
 
The reinstate amount that appears in conversation CAAG is the 
amount that is needed as of the current date.  This amount is not 
necessarily valid in the future because the current bill may be-
come due.

date.

installment schedule.

with the information created with the original agreement.

Create Payment Agreement System Steps- NYC =
Last modified on 05/09/2023 4:26 pm EDT
Summary 
Work Instructions 
Opportunities
Access conversation {CAAG / PF01}
1. Review the credit status main screen
2. Proceed to {PF03}
An the Request Agreement view:
¢ 
Residential - CRIS will default to 15% down payment and 10 months of installments.
° If Minimum DPA, change down payment to $0 and Installment amount to $10.00
° Refer to Residential DPA Guidelines - NYC (https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/residential-dpa-
guidelines-nyc)
¢ Commercial - CRIS will default to pre-populated terms, agent should always determine down payment
based on recent field collection activity.
° Refer to Commercial Deferred Payment Agreements - LIG/NYC/UNY
(https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/new-york-non-residential-deferred-payment-agreements-
lignycuny) for more details.
1. In the {Downpayment Amount} field, enter the negotiated down payment amount.
0 The down payment due date is listed in the {Agrmnt Due Date} field in the upper left column.
2. In the {No Installments} field, delete any pre-populated amount (must be blank to update).
3. In the {Installment Amount} field, delete any pre-populated amount (must be blank to update).
4. Inthe {Action} field, type 0014 (Agreement Offer Mailed).
5. To process agreement {Shift + PF04}

DNY DPA Solutions Email Procedure "
Last modi ed on 01/18/2023 3:58 pm EST
This procedure outlines how to complete the Residential Deferred Payment Agree (DPA) Solution Email for NYC
customers. There are 2 options within the Solutions Email:
1. Verbal Consent:
Submit the Solutions email (https://nationalgridus.tfaforms.net/wfnjYgz) following the steps on the
Verbal Consent tab.
2. Signature:
Submit the Solutions email (https://nationalgridus.tfaforms.net/wfnjYgz) following the steps on the
Signature tab.
For details about NYC Residential DPA options, please review the Residential DPA Guidelines - NYC
(https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/magnyc-payment-agreements-residential-residential-dpa-guidelines-nyc)

Reinstate Payment Agreement Procedure - MAG/NYC "
Last modi ed on 05/05/2023 10:41 am EDT
This procedure explains how to reinstate a Payment Agreement for a MAG/NYC account.
A Payment Agreement will default if:
Down Payment is not received by the down payment due date OR
Monthly payment (installment amount plus current charges) is not received by the bill due date.
A Payment Agreement can be reinstated if:
A payment is received that is equal or greater than the reinstate amount.
Content Owner
Mallory Schaffer - Credit & Collections

Reinstate Payment Agreement Procedure - MAG/NYC
Last modified on 05/05/2023 10:41 am EDT
Summary 
Work Instructions
1. Use Conversation {CAAG}. Go to {PFO2/UPDT}.
2. Advise COR /SCOR the amount in the {Reinstate Amount Field}.
o This amount is as of the current date.
o Amount may not be valid in the future because the current bill may become due.
3. Advise the customer of the {Current Charges} that are due by the Bill Due Date to ensure the agreement does
not default again.
4, Payment Agreement can be reinstated if customer:
o Made payment through IVR and payment is pending, proceed to step 5.
o Makes a payment over the phone: Follow Ways to Pay Procedure
(https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/ways-to-pay-procedure)
REMINDER: National Grid no longer processes credit and debit card payments over the
phone with an agent. Follow the Pay Bill by Credit/Debit Card Procedure
(https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/pay-bill-by-card) for more information.
= Ifcustomer pays over the phone, proceed to step 5.
= Ifcustomer does not pay over the phone, advise that the Payment Agreement will remain in
defaulted status until the payment has been made and they call back in to reinstate the agreement
if the reinstate option is still available.
=» Ifcustomer is unable to pay reinstatement amount, refer to DPA Guidelines to see next option for
customer:
» Residential DPA Guidelines - MAG (https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/magnyc-payment-
agreements-residential-residential-dpa-guidelines-mag)
» Residential DPA Guidelines - NYC (https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/magnyc-payment-
agreements-residential-residential-dpa-guidelines-nyc)
=» 
NOTES:
= CRSwill NOT Auto-Reinstate the Payment Agreement when the payment posts to the
account.
» Advise the customer that a mailed in payment may take 7-10 business days and may not be
received in time to reinstate the agreement.
5. If customer paid reinstate amount with agent over phone or CSR can see the IVR payment pending, in the
{Action} field, type the code 0003 for Reinstate Defaulted Agreement.
6. Select {PF 16} to process.
S) Opportunities
e Paperless billing option (https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/paperless-billing-procedure)

Residential DPA Guidelines - NYC "
Last modi ed on 05/25/2023 3:31 pm EDT
This procedure explains the Deferred Payment Agreement (DPA) Jurisdictional Regulations for Active and Locked Non-Pay (LNP)
Residential New York City (NYC) accounts.
Reminder: Always summarize the DPA terms for the customer - including down payment and due date, installment amount and
number of installments, and monthly bill will include current charges 
 DPA installment. 
General Information:
Customer can continue to be eligible for a new Specific/Standard Terms or Minimum Terms DPA if they have not defaulted
on a previous agreement within the last 18 months.
NOTE: Any COVID or Interim DPA that was made between March 2020 - April 2022 will not count towards the defaulted
agreements. 
If there is a significant change in financial situation from the start date of the previously defaulted DPA, customer can
provide reason and proof of hardship in order to renegotiate the Payment Agreement.
Customer will need to complete a Financial Statement to determine potential eligibility. 
Send the customer the Financial Statement Calculator Solutions Email
(https://nationalgridus.tfaforms.net/401) to complete it on their own or
 Backoffice will add the DPA if the customer is eligible.
Advise customer to complete a Financial Statement at walk-in office
Send customer Financial Statement Proof Solutions Email (https://nationalgridus.tfaforms.net/761)
which will provide them with examples of proof they can bring with them to the walk-in office as well
as walk-in office locations.
If the customer has defaulted on a Specific/Standard Terms DPA, they are not eligible for another Specific/Standard
Terms DPA for a period of 18 months from the date of default.
Customer would still be eligible for Minimum Terms DPA if they qualify financially.
Customer will need to complete a Financial Statement to determine potential eligibility. 
Send the customer the Financial Statement Calculator Solutions Email
(https://nationalgridus.tfaforms.net/401) to complete it on their own or
 Backoffice will add the DPA if the customer is eligible.
Advise customer to complete a Financial Statement at walk-in office
Send customer Financial Statement Proof Solutions Email
(https://nationalgridus.tfaforms.net/761) which will provide them with examples of proof they
can bring with them to the walk-in office as well as walk-in office locations.
If the customer defaulted on a General DPA they are still eligible for a Specific/Standard Terms DPA.
If customer insists on paying more than 15% down with less than 10 installments, supervisor approval is required to
establish the DPA.
If supervisor approves, use ASRQ to state "SUP OK DPA TERMS".
Direct Pay/EFT is not permitted with a Deferred Payment Agreement (DPA). Refer to Direct Pay & DPA Talking Points
(https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/direct-pay-dpa-talking-points) for more details.
DPA Mailing Address and Email (if requested):
National Grid
300 Erie Blvd West
Syracuse, NY 13202
Attn: AMO Protections Team, C-1
DNYPROOF@nationalgrid.com
Content Owner
Aramis Cornier & Mallory Schaffer - Credit & Collections

Residential DPA Guidelines- NYC =
Last modified on 05/25/2023 3:31 pm EDT
Summary 
DPA Terms Overview 
Work Instructions 
Communications
Active (Service On) 
LNP (Service Off)
No previously defaulted Specific/Standard or Minimum 
No previously defaulted Specific/Standard or Minimum
DPA within the last 18 months 
DPA within the last 18 months
Defaulted Specific/Standard Terms DPA 
Defaulted Specific/Standard Terms DPA
Defaulted Minimum Terms DPA 
Defaulted Minimum Terms DPA
LNP (Service Off) NOTES:
¢ 
If the customer claims they are awaiting HEAP, DOSS or any other agency payments, we must wait for that grant before
the service can be restored.
e 
If the customer claims they have a Medical Condition, we must have that documentation before the service can be
restored.
e Always check to see if the customer owes us any other balances from previous addresses. This is our chance to collect
that money.
Active (Service On)
No previously defaulted Specific/Standard or Minimum DPA within the last 18 months

(https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/heap-tip-sheet-for-active-accounts-for-new-york-state) to determine if the
customer may be eligible for HEAP prior to setting up a DPA.
¢

Emergency HEAP, which historically opens in January.

Viewing Payment Agreements - CSS System Steps "
Last modi ed on 05/10/2023 7:45 am EDT
If there is an active Payment Agreement on an account, the Collection Status on the Bill Account window will show
Payment Agreement. From the Bill Account window use menu path to access the payment agreement dialog.

5 - Collection Arrangement LIG 
Last modified by Reg Henderson on 03/26/2021 9:05 pm EDT 
 
 
 
A collection arrangement is a short-term rescheduling of payments for the full arrears and/or current bill that goes 
beyond the current due date. National Grid must effectively manage customer account arrears. Collection 
Arrangements have been established to enhance customer service and assist the customer with the maintenance of 
their gas account. 
 
CSS will offer from 36 to 42 days to schedule payments for residential accounts 
 
There are three installment options available for Collection Arrangements 
One Installment Arrangement: used when the customer is going to make payment in full on a specific date 
Multiple Installment Arrangements: used when the customer is going to make multiple weekly or bi- 
weekly payments 
Calendar Arrangement: used when the customer is going to make scheduled payments within a maximum 
duration of 42 days 
 
Collection activity will stop for the time specified on the arrangement 
Prevents collection action from occurring if payments are received as scheduled 
Payments need to be scheduled for the full amount of the arrears 
CSS reviews all arrangements and defaults on the first missed payment 
Late Payment Charges will continue to be charged on the past due amount 
Commercial accounts may be allowed to have a collection arrangement for up to 6 months. This extended 
calendar is intended for Managed commercial accounts and should be used only at the discretion of the Account 
or Collection Manager. 
Collection Arrangement and Payment Agreement cannot exist on a bill account at the same time 
Previously defaulted Collection Arrangements require 25% down payment before a new arrangement can be 
issued 
 
 Key Functions and Features: 
 
When there is an active Collection Arrangement on an account, the Collection Status on the Bill Account window will 
show Collections Arrangement. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
There are several ways to access the Collections Arrangement dialog. From the Bill Account window use menu path, 
use the Collections Arrangement icon at the bottom of the bill account window or the Hot Key.

The Details button on Maintain Collection Arrangement window will open the Display Collection Arrangement

Display Collection Arrangement Details window 
 
This window displays the details of an existing Collection Arrangement. 
 
Issued: Displays the date the collection arrangement was 
originally created 
 
Last Reviewed: Displays the date the collection 
arrangement was last reviewed 
 
Review Status: The current status of the collection 
arrangement 
Active 
Defaulted 
Blank when creating a new arrangement 
 
Last Amended: Date when the arrangement was last 
modified 
 
Amendments: Number of times the arrangement has been modified 
 
Installment details: 
 
Date: Date the installment was due or paid 
 
 Note: Three business days should have been manually added to the date the customer stated they will be sending in 
their payment. This allows time for processing the payment. 
 
Type: Shows if the installment was paid or is still due. When displaying Not Reviewed it is waiting for the due 
date to check if payment has been received. 
Amount($): The dollar amount owed or received

 Contacts for Collection Arrangements: 
 
Customer contact information is critical for Collection Arrangements. If the customer defaults or the arrangement is 
changed, canceled or completed, the Issued Collection Arrangement Contact would be the only record to contain the 
details of the original arrangement or any changes. 
 
To access the Collection Arrangement contact, select the Contact Icon on the Bill Account window 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Double click on the Issued Collection Arrangement contact to access the contact information for the Collection 
Arrangement. 
 
When a Collection Arrangement is created the contact type is predetermined by CSS. 
 
The contact should include: 
 
Name of the person agreeing to the arrangement 
Exact payment amounts 
Due dates 
 
The due dates should be the ones the customer stated. 
 
Remember, three business days have been added to the dates in CSS to allow time to process the payment. 
 
To close this window, use menu Path: { Contact / Exit } or select the X in the upper right hand corner. 
 
 
 
 
 
From the Bill Account window use menu Path: { Actions / Account Actions / Collections / Maintain Collection 
Arrangement }

6 - Payment Agreements LIG 
Last modified by Reg Henderson on 03/26/2021 9:05 pm EDT 
 
 
 
A Payment Agreement gives the customer the option to pay arrears plus the current bill or the average monthly bill 
(budget) over a longer period of time. 
 
The system knows what the current charges are, the past due charges are and if there are any suspended 
charges. It calculates the payment agreement based on this information. 
Collection activities stop when the Payment Agreement is created 
Late Payment Charges stop when the customer is on a Payment Agreement 
When a customer defaults on a Payment Agreement, collection activity will immediately resume 
When a Payment Agreement defaults, CSS will automatically start accessing Late Payment Charges on the 
account 
The down payment due date is ten days from the date of creation and will default if not received within that time 
The system will automatically restore a Payment Agreement when a payment is posted equal to the restore 
amount and the account has not been cut out for non payment 
A Payment Agreement and a Collection Arrangement cannot exist on an account at the same time 
An active Payment Agreement needs to be manually canceled before creating a Collection Arrangement 
An active Collection Arrangement will automatically cancel when a Payment Agreement is created 
 
Key Functions and Features: 
 
If there is an active Payment Agreement on an account, the Collection Status on the Bill Account window will show 
Payment Agreement. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
From the Bill Account window use menu path to access the payment agreement dialog. 
 
 
 
Maintain Payment Agreement: 
 
This window will display current payment agreement information as well as historical agreement information. 
Path: { Actions / Account Actions / Collections / Maintain 
Payment Agreement }

7- Payment Agreement Guidelines LIG 
Last modified by Reg Henderson on 01/26/2021 2:21 pm EST 
 
 
 
If a customer has no previously defaulted payment agreement (or the last agreement is refused), offer a “Negotiated 
Standard” payment agreement. No financial statement is required. 
 
Never change the terms of the “Negotiated Standard” payment agreement. 
A “new” script must be read prior to completing all financial statements. 
Customers are required to verify their financial statement by providing documentation of income and expenses 
before being offered a “Minimum” payment agreement. 
 
Always ask the customer if they can make a payment today. Offer to take a free Check by Phone payment, if the 
customer would like to pay by Debit or Credit card advise that we unable to process over the phone with an agent, but 
we can transfer them to Western Union to make a payment. 
 
 
 
Residential Collections (DPA) Guidelines : 
 
 
Suggested Language regarding payment options: 
 
We offer three repayment programs: 
 
1. A short-term Collection Arrangement that will assist with paying the balance in full within 36 to 40 days with flexible 
installment amounts you choose, and will require a 25% down payment to start. Late payment charges of 1.5% per month will 
continue to apply. 
 
2. A Standard Deferred Payment Agreement is a longer-term monthly repayment plan which requires 15% of your full balance 
as a down payment , the remaining balance to be paid in equal monthly installments, plus your current bill each month. This 
agreement requires you pay the down payment, sign and return the agreement within 10 days in order for it to be activated. 
 
3. A More Lenient Deferred Payment Agreement (Minimum) is offered to customers who are able to demonstrate financial 
need. A down payment is not required and installments may be as low as $10 per month plus your current bill. To apply for a 
more lenient deferred payment agreement, you will need to provide documentation of your income and allowable household 
expenses. 
 
Please read the following script prior to completing every financial statement: 
 
“In an effort to assist you with payment options, I would like to complete a financial statement by asking you a series of 
questions regarding your income and expenses. Please provide actual figures from the last 30 days as you will be required to 
send supporting documents.” 
 
 
To Add a new Payment Agreement, use the menu path to access the Maintain Payment Agreement dialog. 
 
Path: { Actions / Account Actions / Collections / Maintain Payment Agreement } 
 
Add a Payment Agreement:

Select the Add button 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Select the Payment Agreement Type from the drop down box. CSS will only display valid offers based on the 
customers credit rating and history. 
 
Based on the type of Payment Agreement selected, CSS may prefill the necessary field, or they may have to be 
entered manually. 
 
Down Payment: Enter amount to be paid as a down payment. CSS may automatically calculate the installment 
amounts and number of installments. 
 
Installment Amount: Enter the desired installment amount, CSS will calculate the number of installments 
 
Number of Installments: Enter the number of installments, CSS will calculate the installment amount. 
 
Do Not Send Form: Payment Agreements require a signature from the customer of record. A form is 
automatically sent to the customer unless this box is selected.

Enter appropriate comment detailing the payment agreement. 
 
Select OK to continue 
Select OK again to continue

An informational message will confirm the payment agreement was processed. Select OK to continue.

DocuSign DPA Procedure - LIG 
Last modified on 05/10/2023 7:47 am EDT
DocuSign DPA Procedure
DocuSign Talking Points
(Electronic signature capture)
Available for Account Initiation, Cut In and Active Minimum DPA’s.
Faster as the customer does not need to print the deferred payment agreement to sign and send back.
Email delivery only, so be sure to capture the customers email accurately, restate customer’s email back to
them for confirmation.
Customer will need to perform SMS Authentication to open and sign the DPA. 
1. Select the appropriate Deferred Payment Agreement, by selecting either
a - Active DPA / AI DPA / Cut In DPA
     
        2. Complete all the required fields and then click submit. (Important Reminders) The “Customer Phone” field
will need to be the customer’s  working cell phone number! (This is the number that will be used for the required
customer SMS authentication.
        a - Confirm customer email to prevent any errors in customer receiving the DocuSign DPA email.
      3. Customer will receive the DocuSign DPA email and click on “Review Document”.
Testimony of William D. Yates, CPA, Cases 23-G-0225, 23-G-0226 
Exhibit ___(WDY-05) 
Page 61 of 220


--- Page 62 ---
 
Customer Steps/Screens:
Customers will need to authenticate their cell phone number to access the DPA and to electronically sign it with
DocuSign.
1. Customer will click on “Send SMS” 
2. Customer’s cell phone number will receive authentication code to enter in and click on “Confirm Code”.

Customer with then click on the “Sign” button to electronically sign the DPA.
       7. Customer will need to enter their “Full Name” and “Initials” then click on “Adopt and Sign”.
       8. Customer will finalize by clicking on “Finish”
     
       9. Customer will be presented a completion screen, notifying them they are done and they will click on
“Continue”.
       10. Final screen confirming customer has electronically signed their DPA successfully.

Financial Statement Procedure - LIG 
Last modified on 05/08/2023 2:06 pm EDT
This procedure provides instructions to complete or update a financial statement for Long Island Gas
(LIG) customers seeking a Minimum DPA.
Reminders: 
If Financial Statement is dated within the last 30 days, you can update the existing Financial Statement. Follow
the System Steps for Updating a Financial Statement.
If the customer is claiming a recent financial hardship that is different from the information they provided on
the last Financial Statement, advise the customer to send in supporting documents and they will be used to
make any necessary changes. Do not complete a new Financial Statement.
If customer is CONP, customer is NOT eligible to be cut-in until proof of financials is received and verified
by AMO.

Financial Statement Procedure - LIG 
Last modified on 05/08/2023 2:06 pm EDT
Ask the customer if they would like to complete the Financial Statement online on their own or over the phone with you? 
NOTE: To complete it over the phone, the customer must have all income and expense figures/estimates.
Online - 
Send the customer the Financial Statement Calculator Solutions Email (https://nationalgridus.tfaforms.net/760). They will be able to submit
proof within the online calculator.
Over the phone -
Read the script below to the customer to obtain consent to complete a financial statement:
“In an effort to assist you with payment options, I would like to complete a financial statement by asking you a series of questions regarding
everyone’s income in your household and your household’s allowable expenses. It is very important that we use actual figures from the last 30
days. You will likely be required to provide supporting documents for all incomes and expenses. Do you have those documents / actual figures
available to begin, at this time?
If customer answers no, advise the customer: 
The Financial Statement can't be completed until they have the required documents. 
If they do not want to complete the Financial Statement online, they have to call us back when they have all documents:
Collections Department: 1-800-930-5003
​Hours: 
8:00am - 5:00pm Saturday
8:00am - 8:00pm Monday -Friday
Household Composition
Financial Statement Field
Questions to ask the customer?
Number of Adults
How many adults (age 18 or over) are in the household?
 NOTE:  If discovered account may be eligible for Elderly, Blind and/or Disabled Protection, please
refer to Elderly, Blind and/or Disabled Protection - LIG
(https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/elderly-blind-andor-disabled-ebd-protection-lig).
Number of Children
How many children (under the age of 18) are in the household?
Resources
Financial Statement Field
Questions to ask the customer?
Cash
How much cash do you have on hand that is not intended for other household expenses?
Checking
How much do you have in your checking account that is not intended for other household expenses?
Saving
How much do you have in your savings account that is not intended for other household expenses?
Other Resources
Do you have any other cash or equivalent reserves at this time? Such as stocks, bonds, equity in a
life insurance plan.
Double click on Monthly Income/Expense in the Task List.

Monthly Income
This should cover the total income of ALL household members. Convert weekly or biweekly income to monthly amounts.
Weekly income multiply by 4.3
Bi-weekly income multiply by 2.16
Financial Statement Field
Questions to ask the customer?
Gross Salary
What is the total monthly income of ALL the working members of the household for the previous 30
days?
This would be gross salary minus involuntary deductions such as taxes, social security, union
dues, etc.
Do not deduct voluntary deductions such as a Christmas club/savings account, direct payment
of credit cards, gym membership, etc.
To calculate, ask for net pay and add back in voluntary deductions.
Public Assistance
What is the total direct cash public assistance received by any member(s) of the household?
Social Security
What is the total monthly social security payments received by any member(s) of the household?
Supplemental Security
Income
What is the total monthly Supplemental Social Security or Social Security Disability?  IE. SSI/SSD
Please use the other income section to document more than one individual receiving this type
of income.
 NOTE:  If the customer of record receives SSI or SSI and SSD together (not SSD alone) and has a
shut off notice and is defaulted off of a minimum DPA, DSS should make a 4 month payment that
the customer should not have to pay back.
Unemployment
What is the total monthly unemployment benefit payments received by any member(s) of the
household?
Food Stamps
What is the total monthly amount received for food stamps?
Other Income
Are there any other sources of income received by any member(s) of the household? IE. Rental
properties, pensions, annuities, alimony support, child support, other familial support, etc.
Testimony of William D. Yates, CPA, Cases 23-G-0225, 23-G-0226 
Exhibit ___(WDY-05)

DSS Monthly Expenses
Financial Statement Field
Questions to ask the customer?
Shelter
How much do you pay for rent or mortgage?
Households receiving rent subsidies (HUD, Section 8) should list actual rent paid as the expense
unless the subsidy was previously included as a form of income.
Real Estate Tax
If taxes are not included in the mortgage payment, how much do you pay for real estate tax?
You may need to divide this figure by 12 as it is likely an annual amount.
Home Owners / Rental
Insurance
If not already included in the mortgage or rent payment, how much do you pay for home owners
insurance or rental insurance?
Utilities
This is pre-filled in with National Grid average monthly bill.
Other Fuel
What other fuel expenses do you have in addition to National Grid? IE. fuel oil, wood, coal, kerosene,
propane, gas (if provided by another company).
Costs should be represented as an average monthly cost.
Car Payment
What is your monthly payment for your car loan/lease?
Total monthly transportation cannot exceed 20% of total household income. This includes car
payment, car insurance and transportation.
Car Insurance
What is your monthly payment for your car insurance?
Convert yearly or quarterly payments into monthly amounts
Total monthly transportation cannot exceed 20% of total household income. This includes car
payment, car insurance and transportation.
Transportation
What is the average monthly gasoline expenses and regular monthly maintenance performed within
the last 30 days?
Total monthly transportation cannot exceed 20% of total household income. This includes car
payment, car insurance and transportation.
Food/Non-Food
Enter the maximum amount allowed per household by DSS.
Household Size

Each Additional Person
+$282
Personal
Enter the maximum allowable amount times the number of adults and children in the household.
The allowable monthly amount is $45 per person.
Medical
How much do you spend each month out-of-pocket for health care, prescriptions, co-pays, which are
current or on an installment plan. 
Include the average amount the customer pays on a monthly basis for health insurance.
Do not include the total figure of past due medical debts.
Basic Phone
Enter the maximum allowable amount.
The maximum amount is $42 per household.
Child Care
How much do you spend on any essential employment-related child care expenses for the month?
Court Ordered
Do you have any court ordered monthly expenses? IE. Child support, alimony, or garnished wages.

Other Expense
Do you pay for any of the following? If so, how much?
Water/Sewer (if not included in rent)
Trash Removal (if not included in rent)
Laundromat services
Installment or rental payments on major household appliances such as a stove, refrigerator,
washer or dryer
EAF/EAA payback installments
Second mortgage or home equity payments if against the primary residence
If more than one is required, enter "Miscellaneous" in the Other Expense field with the amount
in the Amount box below it.
 
Other Monthly Expenses 
We do not take Long Distance Phone, Credit Card, Miscellaneous Loans or Cable into consideration. Please leave these set to $0.00.
 
No Payment Plan Reason drop down:
Select reason for completing the financial statement
Add a contact on the account indicating that you completed a Financial Statement for the customer and the reason for the Financial Statement.
If completing Financial Statement for Minimum DPA and cash flow is $100 or less:
Advise the customer they will need to submit proof of financials for verification.
Send customer the Financial Statement Proof Solutions Email for the correct account status & region:
Account Initiation Financial Statement of Proof - LIG (https://nationalgridus.tfaforms.net/411?tfa_11=LIG&tfa_6=AI)
Active Financial Statement of Proof - LIG (https://nationalgridus.tfaforms.net/411?tfa_11=LIG&tfa_6=Active)
Cut-In Financial Statement of Proof - LIG (https://nationalgridus.tfaforms.net/411?tfa_11=LIG&tfa_6=Cut-in)
Read the following script to the customer:
"I have emailed you instructions to send proof of your income and expenses to determine your eligibility for a minimum deferred payment agreement.
You can send the documents via fax or email, both are listed on the email you received. We will review your documentation and contact you with the
outcome. Your account will be subject to all applicable collection activity until we receive and verify your documents."
​Once AMO receives the customers Financial Statement Proof, the turnaround time is approximately 48 hours.
NOTES:  If completing the Financial Statement for DSS/HEAP purposes, please DO NOT have customer send in documentation.

Financial Statement Procedure - LIG 
Last modified on 05/08/2023 2:06 pm EDT
 Create a new Financial Statement 
1. In CSS, from the Bill Account window, navigate to the Maintain Financial Statement window. 
{ ACTIONS > ACCOUNT ACTIONS > COLLECTIONS > MAINTAIN FINANCIAL STATEMENT }
2. Select  Add  
3. Complete the Financial Statement using the steps on the Work Instructions tab.
4. Select  Ok  
5. Select { STATEMENT > PROCESS }
6. Select  Ok  
 Update an existing Financial Statement 
1. In CSS, from the Bill Account window, navigate to the Maintain Financial Statement window. 
{ ACTIONS > ACCOUNT ACTIONS > COLLECTIONS > MAINTAIN FINANCIAL STATEMENT }
2. Select the most recent Financial Statement.
3. Select  Change  
4. Update fields that have changed per the customer.
5. Select  Ok  
6. Select { STATEMENT > PROCESS }
7. Select  Ok

Financial Statement Procedure - LIG 
Last modified on 05/08/2023 2:06 pm EDT
 All residential customers applying for a Deferred Payment Agreement are required to provide a financial statement verifying
income and expenses before being offered a Minimum Deferred Payment Agreement. Specific information items required for the
financial statement are listed below. Include your name, phone number and bill account number on copies of the supporting
documentation. Please submit all that are applicable.
To use Navigation Bar, click on the topic to automatically jump to section.
Proof of Income Sources
Proof of No Income
Proof of Expenses
Where to Send Documentation
Proof of Income Sources:
Pay Stub: 4 consecutive weeks 
Letter from Employer: Printed on company letterhead, signed and dated. 
Income Tax Return
Business Records 
Notarized Letter from employer: Must show amount. 
Self Employed: Signed and dated income tax return and all schedules records of earnings and expenses. 
Unemployment Benefits: Award Letter/Benefit Check/Correspondence from NYS 
Private Pension/Annuities: Statement from pension/annuity. 
Social Security: Award Letter/Benefit Check/Correspondence from Social Security Administration. 
Child Support/Alimony: Notarized letter from person providing support, or letter from court, child support/alimony check
stub. 
Workers Compensation: Award Letter/Check stub. 
Veterans Benefit: Award Letter/Benefit check stub/correspondence from Veterans Administration. 
Military Benefit: Award Letter/Check Stub 
Interest/Dividends/Royalties: Statement from financial institution, notarized letter from broker or agent. 
Income from Rent/Room and Board: Notarized letter from roomer/boarder/tenant, rent receipt with date and amount and
address, signed valid lease agreement.
Support from Others: Notarized signed statement from person providing support.
Proof of No Income:
DSS Denial Letter
Notarized letter from source of rent/housing funding
Notarized letter from family member who is paying living expenses
SNAP Denial Letter/Approval Letter
File Federal Form 4506-T
Proof of Expenses:
Shelter: (Mortgage, signed valid lease agreement, notarized landlord statement, rent receipt including current date, amount
and address, SNAP budget sheet, DSS form, HUD/Sec 8). 
Real Estate Tax: Property and school taxes unless included in mortgage. 
Home Owners/Rental Insurance: If the home insurance is included in the mortgage, do not include. Receipt for rental
insurance. 
Utilities (Average Bill): Other Fuel: Provide bill if any other than National Grid. 
Car Payment: Monthly car payment. 
Car Insurance: Copy of statement. Acceptable Forms of Financial Documentation Payment Agreement for Active Account
Payment Agreement on an Active Account Upstate New York 
Medical Expenses: Monthly amount of out-of-pocket expenses for health care, prescriptions which are current and/or are on
an installment payment plan (Do not include the total figure of past due debts). Also, include average amount on a monthly
basis for health insurance.  
Child Care Expenses: Any essential employment-related childcare expenses for the month. 
Court Ordered: Monthly expenses such as child support, alimony, or garnished wages. 
Other Expenses: Water/sewer and trash removal (if not included in the rent), laundromat services, installment or rental
payments on major household appliances such as a stove, refrigerator, washer/dryer. EAF/EAA payback installments, second
mortgage or home equity payments if against the primary residence. 
Where to submit documentation:
Active Account:
Email: LIDPA@nationalgrid.com (mailto:LIDPA@nationalgrid.com)
Fax: 315-460-8015
Cut-Out Account:
Email: LIDPACI@nationalgrid.com (mailto:LIDPACI@nationalgrid.com)
Fax: 315-460-9841
Account Initiation Account:
Email: LIDPAAI@nationalgrid.com (mailto:LIDPAAI@nationalgrid.com)
Fax: 315-460-9845

Summary
Review the breakdown of what the Payment Agreement screen sections in CSS are and the details they provide. 
NOTE Select the Cancel button to close this window or select the X in the upper right hand corner
View Payment Agreement Window
{Actions > Account Actions > Collections > Maintain Payment Agreement}
Type -  Type of agreement (vary by state)
Status -
Active = Current
Restored = Payment received late and
agreement was reinstated.
Canceled = Manually canceled
Refused = Signed agreement not received
Defaulted = Customer did not pay as
agreed
Restored Defaulted = Customer did not
pay as agreed on a restored agreement
Completed = Paid in full
Restored Completed = Paid in full
Agreement Details
Date Started - Date agreement was created
Agreement Amount - Payment agreement dollar amount
Downpayment Details
Due Date - Date down payment is due by (10 days from creation
date)
Downpayment Amount - Amount of down payment
Restore Amount - Amount due to restore a defaulted payment
agreement
Current charges - Current amount due
Installment Details
Installment Amount - Amount of monthly installment
Number of Installments - Total number of installments
scheduled for this payment agreement
Final Installment Amount - Amount of last installment
Remaining Installments - Number of installments that have
not been billed

Payment Agreements in Excess of 999 months - LIG 
Last modified on 05/10/2023 7:46 am EDT
If a customer owes more than $9,990 and is being offered a Minimum Deferred Payment Agreement with $10
per month installments, CSS will not accept the DPA for more than 999 months.

Payment Agreements in Excess of 999 months - LIG 
Last modified on 05/10/2023 7:46 am EDT
1. Manually calculate the arrears over $9,990.
 
2. Add Suspend Type (https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/suspended-charge-procedure-lig): Other
Suspend Dollar Amount: Arrears in excess over $9,990 
Suspend Until Date: 12/31/64
Comments: "Suspend 999 for DPA over 999 months"
 
3. Add the Minimum Payment Agreement (https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/minimum-payment-agreement-
system-steps-css-lig).
 
4. Advise customer:
If the Payment Agreement has been canceled or defaulted, the Suspend will be canceled and the whole
balance will become due.
If defaulted, a letter is mailed advising the customer to make the required payment to automatically
restore the agreement. 
The Suspend will be canceled on the Next Read date if the payment has not posted.
Once the Suspend is canceled, the agreement will no longer automatically restore because the
suspended dollar amount is now included in the restore amount.
 Summary  
Work Instructions

this payment agreement.

--- Page 82 ---
Update the Financial statement in CSS based on what was received. {Actions/ Account
Actions/Collections/Maintain Financial Statement}
Review the date of the financial statement.  If it is 30 days or older, then the customer needs to be
contacted to call collections to set up a new statement.
If the date is less than 30 days, click change to update the customer's financial statement
Click Monthly Income/Expenses
All fields must be updated, except for:
Food/non Food
Personal
Utilities average bill
Basic Phone
Testimony of William D. Yates, CPA, Cases 23-G-0225, 23-G-0226 
Exhibit ___(WDY-05) 
Page 82 of 220


--- Page 83 ---
When everything in the financial statement has been verified, check to see what the customer’s cash flow is. 
Eligibility 
Customers are eligible if:
A negative cash flow or a positive cash flow of LESS than $100 
Contact the customer and see how they would like it sent Email/Fax/DocuSign/Mail. 
Use CSS to set up a minimum DPA – leave it in pending status and if the customer does not want
the DPA form by mail click on the "do Not Send Form" box.
Add General Collections Contact on the account, notifying that the customer is eligible for a
minimum DPA. (See Activate DPA Article for more information)
Customers are not eligible if:
A positive cash flow greater than $100.  ​A contact should be put on stating such.
A General Collections contact should be placed on the account notifying the customer of the
ineligibility.
What to Say
For every call made to the customer, the representative must state:
“Hello. This is [Your Name] from National Grid. Please be advised that this call may be monitored or
recorded to ensure quality service. I am calling you….”
Financial Statement is older than 30 days
State documents were received
Advise initial statement is expired
Advise the customer to contact collections to set up a new statement.
Not all Financial Statement documents received/not accepted
State what documents were received
Advise what documents cannot be accepted and/or missing
The customer qualifies for Minimum DPA
All financial statement docs were received
Customer is eligible for DPA, there is no down payment
There will be additional $10 charge to the customer’s monthly bill until unpaid balance is cured
Customer needs to sign and return the agreement by the date stated on the DPA
The customer does not qualify for a minimum DPA
All financial statement docs were received
State that the customer is not eligible for the minimum DPA
Advise the customer to contact Collections for further options

Reinstatement Calculator System Steps (RALPH) - LIG 
Last modified on 05/10/2023 7:46 am EDT
From the Bill Account Window, access the Payment Agreement Screen:
{ACTION > ACCOUNT ACTIONS > COLLECTIONS > MAINTAIN PAYMENT AGREEMENT} 
Select and highlight the most recent defaulted Payment Agreement   
{COPY} to the clipboard 
Retrieve the Reinstatement Calculator
(https://ccae.na.ngrid.net/coll_web/FormsHub/Form_Calc_Reinstatement)
Click anywhere within the Reinstatement Calculator and press {Control + V} to paste. 
 
From the Bill Account Window, access the Select Bill screen:
{OBJECT > ACCOUNT OBJECT > SELECT BILL} 
Count the number of Bills that the customer has received since the Start Date of the Payment
Agreement through today's date. 
DO NOT count canceled bills.
In the Reinstatement Calculator, type in the number of Elapsed Months:
 
 
In the Bill Account Window:
In the Reinstatement Calculator, type in the Balance Forward from the BAL FWD DUE field

Residential DPA Guidelines - LIG 
Last modified on 05/10/2023 10:48 am EDT
This procedure explains the Deferred Payment Agreement (DPA) Jurisdictional Regulations for Active and Cut-Out Non-Pay
(CONP) Residential Long Island Gas (LIG) accounts.
Reminder: Always summarize the DPA terms for the customer - including down payment and due date, installment amount and
number of installments, and monthly bill will include current charges plus DPA installment. 
General Information:
Customer can only have one defaulted Standard or defaulted Negotiated Standard DPA AND one defaulted Minimum DPA
per Account Collection Cycle.
If the defaulted DPA is on active account:
Look at the View Collection Activity Window.
If the account has been "Removed from Collections Paid" since the defaulted DPA started, the customer is
eligible for a new DPA.
If not eligible, offer to restore prior DPA. 
If no defaulted DPAs on active account:
Customer is eligible for a Negotiated Standard DPA or Minimum DPA if they qualify financially.
NOTE: COVID or Interim DPA that was made between March 2020 - April 2022 will not count towards the one
Standard/Negotiated Standard & one minimum per account collection cycle.
 
The most recent DPA is restorable during any month of the DPA's lifespan.
Example: If a DPA was set up in January and had 12 months of installments, the DPA would be restorable until
December provided the customer pays the correct restore amount. 
If the account is in the billing window and the restore amount is grayed out, calculate the restore amount using the
Reinstatement Calculator (RALPH) (https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/reinstatement-calculator-system-steps-
ralph-lig).
 
Please verify that the account is NOT on a "Direct Voucher Program/Guaranteed Program" (DV/GU) before offering or
setting up a DPA. 
If the account is on DV/GU, the customer is NOT eligible for a DPA.
These programs can be found in the Maintain Assistance Program window {ALT + AALP}.
"DSS Other" is not a program. These customers CAN be offered a DPA.
  
If there is a significant change in financial situation from the start date of the previously defaulted DPA, customer can
provide reason and proof of hardship in order to renegotiate the Payment Agreement. A new Financial Statement would
need to be completed, AMO would review proof and determine if customer is eligible for a new Minimum DPA.
 
Direct Pay/EFT is not permitted with a Deferred Payment Agreement (DPA). Refer to Direct Pay & DPA Talking Points -
LIG (https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/direct-pay-dpa-talking-points-css-lig) for more details.
Content Owner
Mallory Schaffer - Credit & Collections

During Moratorium/Cold Weather Period, if customer has received HEAP within the last 30 days, follow these DPA
steps before proceeding to normal DPA procedures in Step 5.
HEAP DPA Steps - LIG/UNY
Per an agreement with the NYC PSC, notification of a HEAP benefit will either reinstate a deferred payment
agreement (DPA) or a new minimum DPA will be negotiated once all HEAP benefits have been exhausted.
In order to qualify, the customer must call within 30 days from the date the last HEAP pledge was entered
onto the account.
Check the account to confirm they have received all Regular and Emergency HEAP suspends/payments
applicable to them.
If customer has not received all available HEAP, refer them to HEAP Assistance.
Once all HEAP benefits have been exhausted:
If the customer has never had a DPA, follow normal DPA procedures.
 
If the customer has a defaulted Negotiated Standard DPA:
If HEAP covers the restore amount, restore the DPA.
If HEAP does not cover the amount needed to restore the DPA, quote the difference between the restore
amount and the HEAP grant.
If the customer can pay that amount, once it's received, restore the DPA.
If the customer cannot pay the amount and the last HEAP pledge is over 30 days old:
Offer to complete a Financial Statement to determine if the customer is eligible for a Minimum
DPA, follow normal DPA guidelines.
 
If the customer has a defaulted Minimum DPA:
If HEAP covers the restore amount, restore the DPA.
If HEAP does not cover the amount needed to restore the DPA, quote the difference between the restore
amount and the HEAP grant.
If the customer can pay that amount, once it's received, restore the DPA.
If the customer cannot pay that amount, offer the customer a new Minimum DPA.
Advise the customer that the signed DPA contract must be received by the Down Payment Due
Date to remain on the agreement.
Send customer DPA form to sign:
Verbal Consent / Email (to print and sign): Submit Customer Deferred Payment
Agreement Solution Email (https://nationalgridus.tfaforms.net/wf64ORz) 
If customer says yes to Budget Billing - Follow Start Budget Billing - CSS System
Steps (https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/start-budget-billing-system-steps-css)
NOTE: Customer must have an email in order to verbally consent.
Electronic Signature: DPA Electronic Signature (DocuSign) - Active
(https://azitwppusa01.azure.na.ngrid.net/)
Mail: CSS will automatically send via mail when DPA is added
NOTES: 
HEAP opens 11/1/22
E
HEAP
1/3/23

During Moratorium/Cold Weather Period, refer to the HEAP Tip Sheet for Active Accounts
(https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/heap-tip-sheet-for-active-accounts-lig) to determine if the customer may be eligible
for HEAP prior to setting up a DPA.
 
Is customer potentially eligible for HEAP based on the income guidelines?
Yes - Advise the customer of the required information to apply.
DO NOT add a DPA to the account, doing so could jeopardize customer's ability to receive Emergency
HEAP, which historically opens in January.  
No - Proceed to next step.
Does Not Want to Apply - Proceed to next step.
Payment Agreements should NOT be canceled in order to prompt a Disconnect Notice for the customer to
receive EHEAP.
During Moratorium/Cold Weather Period, if customer has received HEAP within the last 30 days, follow
these DPA steps before proceeding to normal DPA procedures in Step 5.
HEAP DPA Steps - LIG/UNY
Per an agreement with the NYC PSC, notification of a HEAP benefit will either reinstate a deferred payment
agreement (DPA) or a new minimum DPA will be negotiated once all HEAP benefits have been exhausted.
In order to qualify, the customer must call within 30 days from the date the last HEAP pledge was entered
onto the account.
Check the account to confirm they have received all Regular and Emergency HEAP suspends/payments
applicable to them.
If customer has not received all available HEAP, refer them to HEAP Assistance.
Once all HEAP benefits have been exhausted:
If the customer has never had a DPA, follow normal DPA procedures.
 
If the customer has a defaulted Negotiated Standard DPA:
If HEAP covers the restore amount, restore the DPA.
If HEAP does not cover the amount needed to restore the DPA, quote the difference between the restore
amount and the HEAP grant.
If the customer can pay that amount, once it's received, restore the DPA.
If the customer cannot pay the amount and the last HEAP pledge is over 30 days old:
Offer to complete a Financial Statement to determine if the customer is eligible for a Minimum
DPA, follow normal DPA guidelines.
 
If the customer has a defaulted Minimum DPA:
If HEAP covers the restore amount, restore the DPA.
If HEAP does not cover the amount needed to restore the DPA, quote the difference between the restore
amount and the HEAP grant.
If the customer can pay that amount, once it's received, restore the DPA.
If the customer cannot pay that amount, offer the customer a new Minimum DPA.
Advise the customer that the signed DPA contract must be received by the Down Payment Due
Date to remain on the agreement.
Send customer DPA form to sign:
Verbal Consent / Email (to print and sign): Submit Customer Deferred Payment
Agreement Solution Email (https://nationalgridus.tfaforms.net/wf64ORz) 
If customer says yes to Budget Billing - Follow Start Budget Billing - CSS System
Steps (https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/start-budget-billing-system-steps-css)
NOTE: Customer must have an email in order to verbally consent.
Electronic Signature: DPA Electronic Signature (DocuSign) - Active
(https://azitwppusa01.azure.na.ngrid.net/)
Mail: CSS will automatically send via mail when DPA is added
NOTES: 
HEAP opens 11/1/22
Emergency HEAP opens 1/3/23
Testimony of William D. Yates, CPA, Cases 23-G-0225, 23-G-0226

Residential DPA Guidelines - LIG 
Last modified on 05/10/2023 10:48 am EDT
  Negotiated Payment Agreement Established Script  
Read this script to the customer when entering them into a Negotiated Standard Deferred Payment Agreement:
"I have entered a Negotiated Standard Deferred Payment Agreement on your account. You have agreed to make a down payment of
(down payment amount) and you will pay the remaining amount on the following schedule (restate monthly installment and number of
months). In order to avoid further collection activity, your down payment must post to your account by (down payment due date). A copy
of your Negotiated Standard Deferred Payment Agreement will be sent to you or made available to you electronically. This agreement
must be signed and returned within 10 calendar days - unless you verbally consented. If we do not received your signed agreement and
down payment on time, the agreement will be considered refused and your account will be subject to all applicable collection activity."

Residential DPA Solutions Email Procedure - LIG 
Last modified on 04/07/2023 10:46 am EDT
Submit the Customer Deferred Payment Agreement Solutions Email (https://nationalgridus.tfaforms.net/wf64ORz)
following the steps below.
NOTES: 
CONP accounts should only be offered Verbal Consent if they are also making the down payment or you can confirm down
payment was made.
Customer must have an email in order to verbally consent.
Call must be recorded in order for customer to verbally consent.
1. Select type of account: Residential
2. Select the Region:  UNY/LI
3. Does the customer want to provide verbal consent for the DPA? Select Yes
4. Push Submit.
5. Complete all fields on the UNY/LI Residential VC DPA Form:
Use copy and paste where appropriate from CSS to the form.
Agreement Amount field = Agreement Amount from Add Payment Agreement Window
Current Bill field = Current Bill amount from Bill Account Window
Down Payment field = Down Payment Amount from Add Payment Agreement Window
Monthly Payment field = Installment Amount from Add Payment Agreement Window
Ask if they would like Budget Billing:
If yes, follow Start Budget Billing - CSS System Steps
(https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/budget-billing-procedure-lig)
Review DPA terms with the customer.
6. If the customer agrees to the terms you detailed for them, check the box for Yes, Customer agrees to the
terms and conditions.
7. Advise the customer that a copy of the DPA will be provided to their email.
8. Push Submit.
Summary

Residential DPA Solutions Email Procedure - LIG 
Last modified on 04/07/2023 10:46 am EDT
Submit the Customer Deferred Payment Agreement Solutions Email
(https://nationalgridus.tfaforms.net/wf64ORz) following the steps below.
1. Select type of account: Residential
2. Select the Region:  UNY/LI
3. Does the customer want to provide verbal consent for the DPA? Select No
4. Does the customer want the DPA emailed to them to print, sign and send back? Select  Yes
5. Push Submit.
6. Complete all fields on the UNY/LI Residential VC DPA Form:
Use copy and paste where appropriate from CSS to the form.
Agreement Amount field = Agreement Amount from Add Payment Agreement Window
Current Bill field = Current Bill amount from Bill Account Window
Down Payment field = Down Payment Amount from Add Payment Agreement Window
Monthly Payment field = Installment Amount from Add Payment Agreement Window
7. Advise the customer that the DPA will be emailed to them and they will need to print, sign and return the
completed form to National Grid at the email or mailing address provided in the email they will receive.
8. Push Submit.

Restore Payment Agreement Procedure - LIG 
Last modified on 04/07/2023 11:12 am EDT
This procedure explains how to restore a Deferred Payment Agreement (DPA) for a LIG account. 
Before proceeding to Work Instructions, review the Residential DPA Guidelines - LIG
(https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/residential-693b432-residential-dpa-guidelines-lig) to determine if customer
should be restoring DPA or establishing a new DPA.
A Payment Agreement will default if :
Down Payment is not received (posted) by the down payment due date OR
Monthly payment (installment amount plus current charges) is not received (posted) by the bill due date.
A Payment Agreement can be restored if:
A payment is received (posted) that is equal or greater than the restore amount
The most recent DPA is restorable during any month of the DPA's lifepan.
Example: If a DPA was set up in January and had 12 installments, the DPA would be restorable until
December provided the customer pays the correct restore amount. 
NOTE: If the account is in the billing window and the restore amount is grayed out, calculate the restore amount using
the Reinstatement Calculator (RALPH) (https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/reinstatement-calculator-system-steps-ralph-
lig).
Content Owner
Mallory Schaffer - Credit & Collections
Last Audit by Content Owner
1/13/23

Restore Payment Agreement Procedure - LIG 
Last modified on 04/07/2023 11:12 am EDT
Summary
Work Instructions
Opportunities
Testimony of William D. Yates, CPA, Cases 23-G-0225, 23-G-0226 
Exhibit ___(WDY-05) 
Page 119 of 220


--- Page 120 ---
1. From the Bill Account Window  {Actions, Account Actions, Collections, Maintain Payment Agreement}
 
2. In the {Maintain Payment Agreement Window}, select the most recent agreement that indicates
Defaulted and click {Change}
 
3. In the {Restore Amount Field} advise COR /SCOR the amount to restore the agreement.
If the account is within the Billing Window (https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/5-day-billing-
cycle-css), the restore amount will not be displayed, calculate a restore amount using the
Reinstatement Calculator (RALPH) (https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/reinstatement-
calculator-system-steps-ralph-lig). 
Be sure to note the account when using the RALPH calculator to determine restore amount.
NOTES:   
The most recent DPA is restorable during any month of the DPA's lifespan.
Example: If a DPA was set up in January and had 12 months of installments, the DPA
would be restorable until December provided the customer pays the correct restore
amount. 
 
4. Advise the customer of the {Current Charges} that are due by the Bill Due Date to ensure the
agreement does not default again.
 
5. Offer to take payment over the phone: Follow Ways to Pay Procedure
(https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/ways-to-pay-procedure-lig) 
 REMINDER:  National Grid no longer processes credit and debit card payments over the
phone with an agent. Follow the Pay Bill by Credit/Debit Card Procedure
(https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/pay-bill-by-debit-credit-card-procedure-lig) for more information.
 
If customer pays over the phone, proceed to step 6.
If customer does not pay over the phone, advise the customer of the following:
The Payment Agreement will remain in defaulted status until the payment has been
received and processed. 
The Restore Amount quoted is good until 4 days before the next bill becomes due. If a new
bill becomes due before the restore amount is posted to the account, a new restore amount
will need to be calculated.
 NOTES:    
CSS will Auto-Restore the Payment Agreement when the payment posts to the
account.
Advise the customer that a mailed in payment may take 7-10 business days and a
check by phone or IVR payment may take 3-5 business days to post to their account.
If customer promises to pay, note the account but agreement should NOT be restored.
 
6.  If customer paid restore amount with agent over phone, change {Status} to Restore Pending.
If the account is in the Billing Window and the Status of the DPA cannot be changed manually and
the customer paid the restore amount over the phone:
Place an Other suspend for 3 business days holding the entire account:
Suspended Charge - CSS System Steps
(https://cite.nationalgridus.com/docs/suspended-charge-procedure-lig)
Advise the customer that CSS will Auto-Restore the Payment Agreement when the payment
posts to the account
 
7. In the {Task List}, add a Customer Contact with details of the call. Click OK.
 
8. In the {Maintain Payment Agreement Window}, select {Agreement, Process}.
Testimony of William D. Yates, CPA, Cases 23-G-0225, 23-G-0226 
Exhibit ___(WDY-05)
